Also load language_records from the same file for per-language metadata\\n7. For each language in the dataset:\\n   a. Load the full UD treebank from HuggingFace (commul/universal_dependencies, split='train')\\n   b. Stream sentences (up to 200 per treebank, as in original dataset)\\n   c. Parse tokens, heads, deprel, upos from each sentence\\n8. Build a dictionary: config_id -> list of sentences, where each sentence = {tokens, heads, deprel, upos}\\n\\n## PHASE 2: Per-Word Phoneme Counting with Epitran (45 min)\\n9. Initialize epitran.Epitran for each language's ISO 639-3 code:\\n   - Map config_id to ISO code (e.g., en_ewt -> 'eng', de_gsd -> 'deu', zh_gsd -> 'zho')\\n   - For scripts not supported by epitran (e.g., some African scripts), flag as 'approximated'\\n10. For each sentence in each language:\\n    a. Try epitranphonemize(token) for each token\\n    b. If epitran succeeds: phoneme_count = len(phonemized_string)\\n    c. If epitran fails (unsupported script/language): fall back to len(token) (character count)\\n    d. Store: per_word_phoneme_counts = [p1, p2, ..., pn] for the sentence\\n    e. Mark the language as 'real' or 'approximated' based on whether epitran succeeded\\n11. Cache results to avoid re-computation if script restarts\\n\\n## PHASE 3: Corrected PID Computation (30 min)\\n12. For each sentence, compute PID for every dependency arc:\\n    a. Parse heads: head_list = [int(h) for h in heads] (1-indexed in UD)\\n    b. For each token j (0-indexed) with head h = head_list[j]:\\n       - If h == 0: skip (root)\\n       - head_pos = h - 1 (convert to 0-indexed)\\n       - dep_pos = j\\n       - min_pos = min(head_pos, dep_pos)\\n       - max_pos = max(head_pos, dep_pos)\\n       - Intervening words = tokens[min_pos+1 : max_pos]\\n       - **CORRECTED PID FORMULA**: pid = sum(per_word_phoneme_counts[k] for k in range(min_pos+1, max_pos))\\n       - This is the total number of phonemes STRICTLY BETWEEN head and dependent\\n    c. Store all PIDs for the sentence\\n13. Compute aggregate statistics per language:\\n    a. mean_pid = mean of all PIDs across all sentences\\n    b. median_pid, std_pid, max_pid\\n    c. pid_distribution (histogram bins)\\n    d. mean_word_distance (original word-based MDD for comparison)\\n    e. functional_vs_lexical split: separate PIDs by deprel type\\n\\n## PHASE 4: Cross-Linguistic Analysis (30 min)\\n14. Build the analysis table: one row per language with:\\n    - language_name, config_id, family, word_order, register\\n    - phoneme_inventory_size (from metadata)\\n    - phonological_density (from metadata)\\n    - mean_word_distance (original MDD)\\n    - mean_pid (new phoneme-space MDD)\\n    - pid_std, pid_max\\n    - n_sentences, n_dependencies\\n    - phoneme_data_quality: 'real' (epitran succeeded) or 'approximated' (character fallback)\\n15. Run statistical tests:\\n    a. Spearman correlation: phonological_density vs mean_pid (full dataset)\\n    b. Spearman correlation: phonological_density vs mean_word_distance (baseline)\\n    c. Spearman correlation: phonological_density vs mean_pid (conservative subsample only)\\n    d. Linear regression: mean_pid ~ phonological_density + word_order (fixed) + family (random)\\n    e. If statsmodels mixed-effects available: use MixedLM with family as random intercept\\n    f. If not: use OLS with family as fixed effects (dummy variables) as approximation\\n16. Compare word-based vs phoneme-based distance rankings:\\n    a. Rank languages by mean_word_distance\\n    b. Rank languages by mean_pid\\n    c. Compute Spearman rank correlation between the two rankings\\n\\n## PHASE 5: Sensitivity Analysis (30 min)\\n17. Split languages into two groups:\\n    a. 'real_phoneme': languages where epitran successfully phonemized\\n    b. 'approximated': languages where character count was used as fallback\\n18. Run all statistical tests separately for each group\\n19. Compare results:\\n    a. Do correlations differ significantly between groups?\\n    b. Are approximated languages outliers?\\n    c. How many languages are in each group?\\n20. Run leave-one-family-out cross-validation:\\n    a. For each language family, train on all other families, test on that family\\n    b. Report whether the null finding holds across families\\n\\n## PHASE 6: Visualization (30 min)\\n21. Generate figures:\\n    a. Scatter plot: phonological_density vs mean_pid (colored by family, sized by n_sentences)\\n    b. Scatter plot: phonological_density vs mean_word_distance (for comparison)\\n    c. Box plot: PID distribution by word_order (SVO vs SOV vs VSO)\\n    d. Bar chart: mean_pid by language family\\n    e. Rank-rank plot: word-distance ranking vs pid ranking\\n    f. Sensitivity comparison: full vs subsample correlation coefficients\\n22. Save figures as both PNG and PDF\\n\\n## PHASE 7: Output (15 min)\\n23. Build method_out.json with structure:\\n    {\\n      \\\"meta\\\": {title, description, n_languages, n_families, date},\\n      \\\"language_results\\\": [per-language stats with both word and pid metrics],\\n      \\\"correlations\\\": {full_dataset, conservative_subsample, by_family},\\n      \\\"regression\\\": {coefficients, p_values, r_squared},\\n      \\\"sensitivity_analysis\\\": {real_phoneme_results, approximated_results, comparison},\\n      \\\"figures\\\": [list of figure file paths],\\n      \\\"hypothesis_test\\\": {prediction, result, conclusion}\\n    }\\n24. Validate output against exp_gen_sol_out schema using aii-json skill\\n25. Generate mini and preview variants\\n26. Log summary of key findings\\n\\n## ERROR HANDLING THROUGHOUT:\\n- If epitran fails for a language: log warning, fall back to character count, flag as 'approximated'\\n- If a treebank fails to load: skip that language, log warning\\n- If mixed-effects model fails: fall back to OLS with family dummies\\n- If any step takes >2 hours: reduce sentence sample from 200 to 100 per treebank\\n- Use ProcessPoolExecutor for parallel phonemization across languages\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"If the primary approach fails at any stage, execute these fallbacks in order:\\n\\n1. **Epitran unavailable or fails for all languages**: If epitran cannot be installed or fails for >50% of languages, fall back to a character-count-based phoneme approximation for ALL languages. This makes the analysis uniform (all approximated) and still tests whether the metric changes rankings. Document this limitation clearly.\\n\\n2. **HuggingFace dataset loading fails**: If commul/universal_dependencies is inaccessible, use the sentence-level data already embedded in full_data_out.json (the `input` field contains tokens and heads). This reduces coverage to the ~200 sentences per language already in the dataset, but is sufficient for the analysis.\\n\\n3. **Mixed-effects models unavailable**: If statsmodels MixedLM is not available or fails, use ordinary least squares with one-hot encoded family dummies. This is less elegant but still controls for phylogenetic non-independence at a coarse level.\\n\\n4. **Time budget exceeded**: If the full 200-sentence-per-language run exceeds 4 hours, reduce to 50 sentences per language.
